# Chemistry — Questions

Answers: [answers.md](answers.md). Question numbers match answer numbers.

## VCE Units 1 & 2

### Unit 1 AOS 1: Elements, bonding and properties

#### Atomic structure and isotopes

**Q1.** How many neutrons are in one atom of chlorine-37 (³⁷₁₇Cl)?
<!-- id: chem12a1-ast-1 -->

- A. 17
- B. 20
- C. 37
- D. 54

**Q2.** Carbon-12 and carbon-14 have the same chemical properties because they have the same
<!-- id: chem12a1-ast-2 -->

- A. mass number.
- B. number of neutrons.
- C. number of nucleons.
- D. number and arrangement of electrons.

**Q3.** The ion ⁵⁶Fe³⁺ contains
<!-- id: chem12a1-ast-3 -->

- A. 26 protons, 30 neutrons and 23 electrons.
- B. 26 protons, 30 neutrons and 29 electrons.
- C. 23 protons, 33 neutrons and 26 electrons.
- D. 26 protons, 56 neutrons and 23 electrons.

**Q4.** A sample of copper contains 69.2% copper-63 and 30.8% copper-65 (take the isotopic masses as 63.0 and 65.0). The relative atomic mass of copper in this sample is closest to
<!-- id: chem12a1-ast-4 -->

- A. 63.0
- B. 63.6
- C. 64.0
- D. 64.4

**Q5.** In Rutherford's gold foil experiment, most alpha particles passed straight through the foil, but a very small number were deflected back. This showed that
<!-- id: chem12a1-ast-5 -->

- A. electrons occupy fixed energy levels.
- B. atoms are solid, indivisible spheres.
- C. an atom is mostly empty space with a small, dense, positively charged nucleus.
- D. negative charges are spread evenly through a positive sphere.

#### Periodic table trends

**Q66.** Moving from sodium to chlorine across period 3, atomic radius decreases. The best explanation is that
<!-- id: chem12a1-pt-1 -->

- A. the number of occupied shells decreases.
- B. core charge increases while the number of occupied shells stays the same.
- C. the number of neutrons increases, pulling electrons closer.
- D. electronegativity decreases across the period.

**Q67.** Which period 3 element has the highest first ionisation energy?
<!-- id: chem12a1-pt-2 -->

- A. Sodium
- B. Magnesium
- C. Chlorine
- D. Argon

<!-- id: chem12a1-pt-3 -->

- A. +2
- B. +6
- C. +10
- D. +16

**Q69.** Potassium reacts more vigorously with water than sodium does. This is mainly because, in potassium,
<!-- id: chem12a1-pt-4 -->

- C. there are more valence electrons.
- D. the atom has a smaller atomic radius.

<!-- id: chem12a1-pt-5 -->

- A. have a higher electronegativity.
- B. have a smaller atomic radius.
- C. be less reactive and have a lower electronegativity.
- D. lose electrons more readily to form positive ions.

#### Metallic bonding and properties of metals

**Q97.** In the metallic bonding model, a metal consists of
<!-- id: chem12a1-mb-1 -->

- A. a lattice of positive ions surrounded by a sea of delocalised electrons.
- B. a lattice of alternating positive and negative ions.
- C. discrete molecules held together by dispersion forces.
- D. atoms that share localised electron pairs with neighbours.

**Q98.** Metals are malleable because
<!-- id: chem12a1-mb-2 -->

- A. the ions are held in fixed positions by directional bonds.
- B. the delocalised electrons evaporate when the metal is struck.
- C. layers of cations can slide over each other without breaking the non-directional metallic bonding.
- D. metals contain weak intermolecular forces between layers.

**Q99.** Which property of metals is NOT well explained by the simple metallic bonding model?
<!-- id: chem12a1-mb-3 -->

- A. Electrical conductivity
- B. Ductility
- C. Thermal conductivity
- D. The magnetic behaviour of iron, cobalt and nickel

**Q100.** A piece of steel is heated until red hot and then allowed to cool slowly in air. This treatment, called annealing, produces steel that is
<!-- id: chem12a1-mb-4 -->

- A. harder and more brittle, with very small crystals.
- B. softer and more ductile, with larger crystals.
- C. harder because carbon atoms leave the lattice.
- D. unchanged in properties.

**Q101.** Metals conduct electricity in the solid state because
<!-- id: chem12a1-mb-5 -->

- A. the cations move towards the negative electrode.
- B. the delocalised electrons can move through the lattice when a potential difference is applied.
- C. metal atoms ionise only when a voltage is applied.
- D. the metal contains free negative ions.

**Q102.** Magnesium melts at 650 °C, whereas sodium melts at 98 °C. Use the metallic bonding model to explain this difference. (2 marks)
<!-- id: chem12a1-mb-6 -->

*Written answer*

**Q103.** Steel is an interstitial alloy of iron containing small amounts of carbon. Explain why steel is harder than pure iron. (2 marks)
<!-- id: chem12a1-mb-7 -->

- C. the sharing of electrons between molecules.
- D. hydrogen atoms attached to nitrogen, oxygen or fluorine.

#### Covalent lattices and carbon allotropes

**Q317.** In diamond, each carbon atom is
<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-1 -->

- A. covalently bonded to three other carbon atoms in flat layers.
- B. covalently bonded to four other carbon atoms in a tetrahedral arrangement.
- C. ionically bonded to four neighbouring carbon ions.
- D. part of a C₆₀ molecule.

**Q318.** Graphite conducts electricity because
<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-2 -->

- A. each carbon atom has one delocalised electron that can move along the layers.
- B. it contains free carbon ions.
- C. dispersion forces between layers carry charge.
- D. all of its valence electrons are held in covalent bonds.

**Q319.** Which substance has a covalent network lattice structure?
<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-3 -->

- A. Carbon dioxide, CO₂
- B. Iodine, I₂
- C. Silicon dioxide, SiO₂
- D. Sodium chloride, NaCl

**Q320.** Graphite is used as a dry lubricant because
<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-4 -->

- A. its covalent bonds are weak.
- B. it has a low melting point.
- C. it reacts with metal surfaces to form a slippery coating.
- D. the weak dispersion forces between its layers allow the layers to slide over each other.

**Q321.** Graphene is
<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-5 -->

- A. a single layer of carbon atoms arranged in hexagons, as in one layer of graphite.
- B. a spherical molecule of 60 carbon atoms.
- C. a three-dimensional tetrahedral carbon network.
- D. an alloy of carbon and iron.

**Q326.** Within a single layer of graphite, the carbon atoms are arranged
<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-10 -->

<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-11 -->

<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-12 -->

- D. C₆₀ is held together by ionic bonding.

<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-13 -->

<!-- id: chem12a1-cl-17 -->

### Unit 1 AOS 2: Quantifying and classifying materials

#### Relative masses and mass spectrometry

**Q349.** Chlorine has two naturally occurring isotopes, ³⁵Cl and ³⁷Cl, and a relative atomic mass of 35.45. Taking the relative isotopic masses as 35 and 37, what is the percentage abundance of ³⁵Cl?
<!-- id: chem12a2-rm-1 -->

- A. 22.5%
- B. 50.0%
- C. 75.5%
- D. 77.5%

<!-- id: chem12a2-rm-2 -->

- C. Electrons removed from the sample, separated according to their energy
- D. Negatively charged ions, separated according to their size

<!-- id: chem12a2-rm-3 -->

- C. the mass of one proton
- D. one-sixteenth of the mass of an atom of oxygen-16

- B. 69.8
- C. 70.0
- D. 70.2

<!-- id: chem12a2-rm-5 -->

- A. 160 is the relative molecular mass of the most abundant isotope of bromine
- B. the ⁸¹Br–⁸¹Br molecule is the most stable

- D. Br₂⁺ ions with m/z 160 are deflected the least

### Unit 2 AOS 1: Chemicals and water

#### Properties of water and hydrogen bonding

- B. water molecules are non-polar

- C. dimethyl ether, because it has a polar C–O bond

- D. A solution with a pH of exactly 7

**Q851.** How should the volume be read from a burette containing a colourless solution?
<!-- id: chem12a3-vt-10 -->

- A. At the top of the meniscus, with the eye above the liquid
- B. At the bottom of the meniscus, with the eye level with the meniscus
- C. At the bottom of the meniscus, with the eye looking down from above
- D. At the top of the meniscus, with the eye level with the meniscus

**Q852.** What is the difference between the end point and the equivalence point of a titration?
<!-- id: chem12a3-vt-11 -->

- A. They are always the same point by definition
- B. The end point is where the reactants are in exact stoichiometric ratio; the equivalence point is where the indicator changes colour
- C. The equivalence point is where the reactants are in exact stoichiometric ratio; the end point is where the indicator changes colour
- D. The end point occurs before the first drop of titrant is added

**Q853.** A 25.00 mL aliquot of HCl is titrated with 0.0800 mol L⁻¹ NaOH. The mean titre is 20.50 mL. What is the concentration of the HCl?

- A. 0.0328 mol L⁻¹
- B. 0.0976 mol L⁻¹
- C. 0.131 mol L⁻¹
- D. 0.0656 mol L⁻¹

**Q854.** Just before a titration, a student rinses the conical flask with deionised water and leaves some water in it. What effect does this have on the calculated concentration of the analyte?
<!-- id: chem12a3-vt-13 -->

- A. No effect, because the amount of analyte in the flask is unchanged
- B. The concentration will be too high, because the titre is larger
- C. The concentration will be too low, because the analyte is diluted
- D. The concentration will be too low, because the titre is smaller

**Q855.** A student rinses the pipette with deionised water, not with the analyte solution, before measuring the aliquot. The calculated analyte concentration will be:
<!-- id: chem12a3-vt-14 -->

- A. too high, because the titre is larger
- B. too low, because the aliquot contains fewer moles of analyte
- C. unchanged, because the pipette volume is fixed
- D. too high, because the water adds to the moles of analyte

**Q856.** An air bubble is trapped in the tip of a burette at the start of a titration and is dislodged and escapes during the titration. How is the calculated analyte concentration affected?
<!-- id: chem12a3-vt-15 -->

- A. Too low, because less titrant was delivered than was read
- B. Unchanged, because air has no mass
- C. Too high, because the measured titre is larger than the volume of titrant actually delivered
- D. Too low, because the titre is smaller than expected

**Q857.** A 20.00 mL aliquot of Na₂CO₃ solution is titrated with 0.100 mol L⁻¹ HCl: Na₂CO₃(aq) + 2HCl(aq) → 2NaCl(aq) + H₂O(l) + CO₂(g). The mean titre is 24.00 mL. What is the concentration of the Na₂CO₃?
<!-- id: chem12a3-vt-16 -->

- A. 0.120 mol L⁻¹
- B. 0.0480 mol L⁻¹
- C. 0.0300 mol L⁻¹
- D. 0.0600 mol L⁻¹

**Q858.** Which substance is the most suitable primary standard for making up a standard acid solution?

- A. Anhydrous sodium carbonate
- B. Solid sodium hydroxide
- C. Concentrated hydrochloric acid
- D. Potassium permanganate

**Q859.** 1.325 g of anhydrous Na₂CO₃ (M = 106.0 g mol⁻¹) is dissolved in water and made up to 250.0 mL in a volumetric flask. What is the concentration of the solution?

- A. 0.0125 mol L⁻¹
- B. 0.0500 mol L⁻¹
- C. 0.00500 mol L⁻¹
- D. 0.0100 mol L⁻¹

**Q860.** Ammonia solution is titrated with hydrochloric acid. Which indicator is more suitable, and why?
<!-- id: chem12a3-vt-19 -->

#### Gravimetric analysis

**Q871.** In gravimetric analysis, the precipitate is repeatedly dried and weighed until it reaches constant mass. This is done to:
<!-- id: chem12a3-ga-1 -->

- A. make the precipitate crystals larger
- B. remove soluble impurities from the precipitate
- C. ensure all water has been removed from the precipitate
- D. convert the precipitate to its oxide

**Q872.** To find the chloride content of a sample, an excess of silver nitrate solution is added. The excess is used to:
<!-- id: chem12a3-ga-2 -->

- A. ensure that all chloride ions are precipitated as AgCl
- B. increase the solubility of AgCl
- C. prevent the AgCl from being washed away
- D. react with any sodium ions present

- B. No effect, because NaCl is soluble
- C. It will be too low, because the precipitate mass decreases
- D. It will be too high, because the dried precipitate mass includes NaCl

#### Colorimetry and UV-visible spectroscopy

**Q902.** A colorimeter is used to find the concentration of a blue copper(II) solution. Which colour of light should be selected, and why?
<!-- id: chem12a3-cu-1 -->

- A. Blue light, because the solution is blue
- B. Orange light, because the solution absorbs it most strongly
- C. White light, because it contains all wavelengths
- D. Green light, because it is in the middle of the visible spectrum

- B. 0.65 mg L⁻¹
- C. 6.2 mg L⁻¹
- D. 1.04 mg L⁻¹

#### Greenhouse gases and ocean acidification

**Q932.** Which of the following gases in the atmosphere is NOT a greenhouse gas?
<!-- id: chem12a3-go-1 -->

- A. Methane, CH₄
- B. Water vapour, H₂O
- C. Nitrogen, N₂
- D. Nitrous oxide, N₂O

**Q933.** Greenhouse gases warm the lower atmosphere mainly because they:
<!-- id: chem12a3-go-2 -->

### Exam-style questions (mixed topics)

**Q962.** Magnesium: atoms, bonds and reactions
<!-- id: vce-chem12-x1 -->
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original question in the style of a Units 1 & 2 end-of-semester exam*

Magnesium, Mg, has atomic number 12. Use M(Mg) = 24.3 g mol⁻¹ and M(O) = 16.0 g mol⁻¹.

**a.** Write the electronic configuration of a magnesium atom using subshell notation. *(1 mark)*

**b.** Magnesium burns in oxygen to form magnesium oxide, MgO. Explain, in terms of electrons, how the ions in MgO form, and why magnesium oxide has a very high melting point. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Calculate the mass of magnesium oxide formed when 4.86 g of magnesium burns completely: 2Mg(s) + O₂(g) → 2MgO(s). *(2 marks)*

Another 4.86 g sample of magnesium is added to excess dilute hydrochloric acid. The magnesium dissolves and bubbles of hydrogen gas form.

**d.** Write a balanced equation, including states, for the reaction. *(1 mark)*

**e.** Identify the species that is oxidised, and write the half-equation for its oxidation. *(2 marks)*

**f.** Calculate the volume of hydrogen gas produced at SLC (25 °C and 100 kPa). The molar volume of a gas at SLC is 24.8 L mol⁻¹. *(2 marks)*

**Q963.** Water, acids and a titration
<!-- id: vce-chem12-x2 -->
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original question in the style of a Units 1 & 2 end-of-semester exam*

A student is investigating the chemistry of water samples.

**a.** Water (H₂O, molar mass 18.0 g mol⁻¹) boils at 100 °C, while hydrogen sulfide (H₂S, molar mass 34.1 g mol⁻¹) boils at −60 °C. Explain this difference in terms of intermolecular forces. *(2 marks)*

**b.** A river water sample has a pH of 6.0 at 25 °C. Calculate the concentration of H₃O⁺ ions, and state whether the sample is acidic, neutral or basic. *(2 marks)*

**c.** A 10.0 mL sample of 0.500 M hydrochloric acid is diluted with water to a total volume of 250.0 mL. Calculate the concentration and the pH of the diluted acid. *(2 marks)*

To find the concentration of a sodium hydroxide solution, the student titrated 20.00 mL aliquots of it with 0.0200 M hydrochloric acid. The average titre was 18.40 mL.

**d.** Write a balanced equation for the reaction between hydrochloric acid and sodium hydroxide. *(1 mark)*

**e.** Calculate the concentration of the sodium hydroxide solution. *(2 marks)*

**f.** State what the burette should be rinsed with just before it is filled, and why. *(1 mark)*

**Q964.** Hydrocarbons and plastics
<!-- id: vce-chem12-x3 -->
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original question in the style of a Units 1 & 2 end-of-semester exam*

Ethene, C₂H₄, is the starting material for polyethene, one of the most widely used plastics.

**a.** Explain why ethene can undergo addition polymerisation. *(1 mark)*

**b.** Name the type of polymerisation and give the repeating unit of polyethene. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Explain the difference between a thermoplastic and a thermoset in terms of their structure and how they behave when heated. *(2 marks)*

A hydrocarbon contains 85.7% carbon and 14.3% hydrogen by mass. Use M(C) = 12.0 g mol⁻¹ and M(H) = 1.0 g mol⁻¹.

**d.** Determine the empirical formula of the hydrocarbon. *(2 marks)*

**e.** The molar mass of the hydrocarbon is 56.0 g mol⁻¹. Find its molecular formula and name one possible structural isomer. *(2 marks)*

**f.** Describe one way the circular economy could reduce plastic waste. *(1 mark)*

**Q965.** Sulfate in fertiliser and a displacement reaction
<!-- id: vce-chem12-x4 -->
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original question in the style of a Units 1 & 2 end-of-semester exam*

To find the sulfate content of a fertiliser, a student dissolved 2.50 g of it in water and added excess barium chloride solution. The barium sulfate precipitate was filtered, washed and dried; its mass was 1.86 g. Use M(BaSO₄) = 233.4 g mol⁻¹ and M(SO₄²⁻) = 96.1 g mol⁻¹.

**a.** Write an ionic equation, with states, for the formation of the precipitate. *(1 mark)*

**b.** Calculate the percentage by mass of sulfate in the fertiliser. *(3 marks)*

**c.** Explain why the precipitate is washed and then dried to constant mass. *(2 marks)*

A strip of zinc is placed in blue copper(II) sulfate solution. Use M(Zn) = 65.4 g mol⁻¹ and M(Cu) = 63.5 g mol⁻¹.

**d.** Write the ionic equation for the reaction and describe two observations. *(2 marks)*

**e.** Explain why no reaction occurs when copper is placed in zinc sulfate solution. *(1 mark)*

**f.** Calculate the mass of copper formed when 0.654 g of zinc reacts completely. *(1 mark)*

### Rates & equilibrium

#### Collision theory and reaction rates

#### Le Chatelier's principle and yield optimisation

**Q1248.** For N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) ⇌ 2NH₃(g), ΔH = −92 kJ mol⁻¹, the volume of the vessel is decreased at constant temperature. How does the system respond?
<!-- id: chem-3-4-1 -->

- A. The equilibrium shifts to the left, because there are more gas particles on the reactant side
- B. There is no shift, because pressure does not affect gaseous equilibria
- C. The equilibrium shifts to the right, because the reaction is exothermic
- D. The equilibrium shifts to the right, because there are fewer gas particles on the product side

**Q1249.** The temperature of an equilibrium mixture of N₂, H₂ and NH₃ (ΔH = −92 kJ mol⁻¹) is increased. Which statement is correct?
<!-- id: chem-3-4-2 -->

- A. The rate and the equilibrium yield of ammonia both increase
- B. The rate decreases but the equilibrium yield of ammonia increases
- C. The rate increases but the equilibrium yield of ammonia decreases
- D. The rate increases and the yield of ammonia is unchanged

**Q1250.** The Haber process is typically run at about 400–450 °C, even though lower temperatures give a higher equilibrium yield. This is because:
<!-- id: chem-3-4-3 -->

- A. the reaction is endothermic, so high temperatures increase yield
- B. the iron catalyst only increases the yield at high temperatures
- C. ammonia decomposes completely below 400 °C
- D. a moderate temperature gives an acceptable yield at a fast enough rate (a compromise)

#### Physical properties of organic compounds

**Q1554.** Which compound has the highest boiling point?
<!-- id: chem34a2-phys-1 -->

- A. Butane, C₄H₁₀
- B. Methyl methanoate, HCOOCH₃
- C. Propan-1-ol, C₃H₇OH
- D. Ethanoic acid, CH₃COOH

**Q1555.** Why do boiling points increase from methane to octane in the alkane homologous series?
<!-- id: chem34a2-phys-2 -->

- A. Hydrogen bonding increases with chain length.
- B. Longer molecules have more electrons and greater surface area, so dispersion forces are stronger.
- C. The C–C covalent bonds become stronger.
- D. Longer alkanes are more polar.

**Q1556.** Pentane boils at 36 °C and its isomer 2,2-dimethylpropane boils at 10 °C. What is the best explanation?
<!-- id: chem34a2-phys-3 -->

- A. 2,2-dimethylpropane has a lower molar mass.
- B. Pentane can form hydrogen bonds.
- C. The compact, branched shape of 2,2-dimethylpropane gives less surface contact, so its dispersion forces are weaker.
- D. Branching makes the C–H bonds weaker.

**Q1557.** Which statement explains why the solubility of alcohols in water decreases from methanol to hexan-1-ol?
<!-- id: chem34a2-phys-4 -->

- A. The longer non-polar hydrocarbon chain disrupts hydrogen bonding with water and cannot itself interact strongly with water.
- B. The –OH group becomes less polar as the chain gets longer.
- C. Larger alcohols react with water.
- D. Larger alcohols have fewer hydrogen atoms.

**Q1558.** Moving from hexane to decane in the alkane series, how do viscosity and flash point change?
<!-- id: chem34a2-phys-5 -->

- A. Viscosity decreases; flash point decreases.
- B. Viscosity increases; flash point decreases.
- C. Viscosity decreases; flash point increases.
- D. Viscosity increases; flash point increases.

### Green chemistry & sustainability

#### Green chemistry principles

**Q1843.** Which statement best describes the green chemistry principle of 'prevention'?
<!-- id: chem-8-1-1 -->

- A. It is better to prevent waste from forming than to treat or clean it up after it has formed.
- B. Hazardous waste should be stored securely on site until it can be incinerated.
- C. All waste products should be diluted before release to the environment.
- D. Pollution controls should be added at the end of a production line.

**Q1844.** A manufacturer replaces dichloromethane with supercritical carbon dioxide as the extraction solvent for decaffeinating coffee. Which green chemistry principle is mainly being applied?
<!-- id: chem-8-1-2 -->

- A. Design for degradation
- B. Use of safer solvents and auxiliaries
- C. Use of renewable feedstocks
- D. Real-time analysis for pollution prevention

**Q1845.** Why are catalysts considered consistent with green chemistry?
<!-- id: chem-8-1-3 -->

- A. They shift the equilibrium position towards products, increasing the equilibrium yield.
- B. They are consumed in the reaction, so no excess reagent is needed.
- C. They lower activation energy, allowing reactions at lower temperatures and are not consumed, reducing energy use and waste.
- D. They increase the enthalpy change of the reaction, releasing more useful heat.

#### Circular economy and sustainability

**Q1921.** Which statement best describes a circular economy?
<!-- id: chem-8-4-1 -->

- A. An economy in which raw materials are extracted, made into products and disposed of after use
- B. An economy that only uses products made from recycled materials
- C. An economy in which materials are kept in use for as long as possible through reuse, repair, remanufacture and recycling, with waste designed out
- D. An economy in which all waste is burned to generate electricity

**Q1922.** A linear economy is best described as:
<!-- id: chem-8-4-2 -->

- A. reduce, reuse, recycle
- B. make, use, recycle, remake
- C. design, use, repair, reuse
- D. take, make, use, dispose

<!-- id: chem-8-4-3 -->

- A. Collecting bottles through the Victorian container deposit scheme and reprocessing them into new food-grade bottles
- B. Sending used bottles to landfill lined with clay
- C. Making bottles thinner so they weigh less
- D. Exporting used bottles to other countries

**Q1924.** Explain the difference between a linear economy and a circular economy.
<!-- id: chem-8-4-4 -->

*Written answer*

**Q1925.** Why is burning a plastic waste to generate electricity considered less circular than recycling it?

- C. Recycling releases more CO₂ than burning.

<!-- id: chem-8-4-6 -->

- C. They soften on heating and can be remoulded, because there are only weak forces between chains and no cross-links.
- D. They do not melt at any temperature.

<!-- id: chem-8-4-7 -->

- B. shredded and buried in landfill.
- C. burned to generate electricity.
- D. broken down into monomers or small molecules that can be used to make new polymers or chemicals.

### Exam-style questions (mixed topics)

**Q1957.** Ethanol as a fuel
<!-- id: chem-x1 -->
*Multi-part question · 13 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Chemistry exam, Section B*

Bioethanol, C₂H₅OH, is made by fermenting glucose from plant material and can be burnt as a fuel. The molar heat of combustion of ethanol is 1360 kJ mol⁻¹. Use M(C₂H₅OH) = 46.0 g mol⁻¹.

**a.** Write a balanced thermochemical equation, including states, for the complete combustion of ethanol. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Calculate the energy released, in kJ, when 5.00 g of ethanol burns completely. *(2 marks)*

A student burnt 1.20 g of ethanol in a spirit burner under a can holding 250 g of water. The temperature of the water rose from 20.0 °C to 41.5 °C. The specific heat capacity of water is 4.18 J g⁻¹ °C⁻¹.

**c.** Calculate the student's experimental value for the molar heat of combustion of ethanol, in kJ mol⁻¹. *(3 marks)*

**d.** Give one reason why the experimental value is lower than 1360 kJ mol⁻¹. *(1 mark)*

Ethanol can be oxidised to ethanoic acid, CH₃COOH. Ethanoic acid then reacts with more ethanol, with a little concentrated sulfuric acid as a catalyst, to form an ester.

**e.i.** Name a reagent that oxidises ethanol to ethanoic acid. *(1 mark)*

**e.ii.** Give the IUPAC name of the ester formed, and name the type of reaction that forms it. *(2 marks)*

**f.** Fermentation is represented by C₆H₁₂O₆(aq) → 2C₂H₅OH(aq) + 2CO₂(g). Calculate the percentage atom economy for making ethanol by fermentation. Use M(C₆H₁₂O₆) = 180.0 g mol⁻¹. *(2 marks)*

**Q1958.** Green hydrogen: making it and using it
<!-- id: chem-x2 -->
*Multi-part question · 12 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Chemistry exam, Section B*

Green hydrogen is made by electrolysing water using electricity from renewable sources. In one electrolyser the electrolyte is acidic. Use F = 96 500 C mol⁻¹ and M(H₂) = 2.0 g mol⁻¹.

**a.** Write the half-equation for the reaction at the cathode of the electrolyser. *(1 mark)*

**b.** A current of 50.0 A passes through the electrolyser for 2.00 hours. Calculate the mass of hydrogen gas produced. *(3 marks)*

The hydrogen is used in a fuel cell that also has an acidic electrolyte.

**c.** Write the half-equation for the reaction at the anode of the fuel cell, and the overall equation for the cell. *(2 marks)*

**d.** State one way in which a fuel cell differs from a primary galvanic cell such as an alkaline battery. *(1 mark)*

Hydrogen is also used to make ammonia: N₂(g) + 3H₂(g) ⇌ 2NH₃(g), ΔH = −92 kJ mol⁻¹. At a fixed temperature, a 2.00 L vessel at equilibrium contains 0.400 mol N₂, 1.20 mol H₂ and 0.300 mol NH₃.

**e.i.** Write the expression for the equilibrium constant, \(K\), for this reaction. *(1 mark)*

**e.ii.** Calculate the value of \(K\) at this temperature. *(2 marks)*

**f.** The temperature of the vessel is increased. Explain the effect on the equilibrium yield of ammonia. *(2 marks)*

**Q1959.** Identifying an acid and making an ester
<!-- id: chem-x3 -->
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Chemistry exam, Section B*

An unknown compound X has the molecular formula C₃H₆O₂. Its infrared spectrum has a very broad absorption from 2500 to 3300 cm⁻¹ and a sharp, strong absorption near 1710 cm⁻¹.

**a.** Identify the functional group in X, referring to both absorptions. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Give the IUPAC name of X. *(1 mark)*

**c.** Predict the m/z value of the molecular ion peak in the mass spectrum of X. *(1 mark)*

**d.** How many different hydrogen environments are there in X, and what splitting pattern would the CH₃ signal show in its ¹H NMR spectrum? *(2 marks)*

X reacts with methanol, using concentrated sulfuric acid as a catalyst, to form an ester.

**e.** Write a balanced equation for the reaction and name the ester. *(2 marks)*

**f.** 7.40 g of X produced 6.16 g of the ester. Calculate the percentage yield. Use M(ester) = 88.0 g mol⁻¹. *(2 marks)*

**Q1960.** A lead–acid battery and a catalyst
<!-- id: chem-x4 -->
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Chemistry exam, Section B*

A car battery is a lead–acid cell. While it discharges, the overall reaction is Pb(s) + PbO₂(s) + 2H₂SO₄(aq) → 2PbSO₄(s) + 2H₂O(l).

**a.** Write the half-equation for the reaction at the anode during discharge. *(2 marks)*

**b.** What type of cell is the battery while it is being recharged, and why does recharging work? *(1 mark)*

**c.** Explain why it is important that the PbSO₄ formed during discharge stays on the electrodes. *(1 mark)*

The battery delivers a steady current of 5.00 A for 30.0 minutes. Use F = 96 500 C mol⁻¹ and M(Pb) = 207.2 g mol⁻¹.

**d.** Calculate the amount, in mol, of electrons transferred. *(2 marks)*

**e.** Calculate the mass of lead consumed at the anode. *(2 marks)*

Hydrogen peroxide decomposes slowly into water and oxygen. Adding a little manganese dioxide, MnO₂, makes the reaction much faster without the MnO₂ being used up.

**f.** Use collision theory to explain how the catalyst increases the rate of reaction. *(2 marks)*

**Q1961.** Challenge: methanol as a fuel
<!-- id: chem-cx1 -->
*Challenge question*
*Multi-part question · 12 marks · Original challenge question in the style of the VCE Chemistry exam, Section B*

Methanol, CH₃OH (M = 32.0 g/mol), can be burnt or used in a fuel cell. Its molar enthalpy of combustion is −726 kJ/mol.

**a.** Write a balanced thermochemical equation for the complete combustion of methanol, with states. *(2 marks)*

In a school experiment, burning 1.20 g of methanol heated 200.0 g of water from 18.5 °C to 32.0 °C.

**b.** Calculate the experimental molar enthalpy of combustion. *(3 marks)*

**c.** Calculate the percentage of the energy released that was absorbed by the water, and give one reason it is low. *(2 marks)*

**d.** In a methanol fuel cell with an acidic electrolyte, write the half-equations at the anode and the cathode. *(2 marks)*

**e.** The fuel cell converts 50% of the chemical energy to electrical energy. How much electrical energy can 1.00 kg of methanol supply? *(2 marks)*

**f.** Give one advantage of using methanol in a fuel cell rather than burning it. *(1 mark)*

**Q1962.** Challenge: making and analysing an ester
<!-- id: chem-cx2 -->
*Challenge question*
*Multi-part question · 13 marks · Original challenge question in the style of the VCE Chemistry exam, Section B*

Ethanoic acid reacts with ethanol, with a sulfuric acid catalyst, to form an ester and water. At the temperature used, K = 4.0 for this equilibrium.

**a.** Name the ester and write the equation, using condensed structural formulas. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Write the expression for K, and explain why the volume of the mixture does not affect its value here. *(2 marks)*

**c.** 1.00 mol of acid and 1.00 mol of ethanol are mixed. Calculate the amount of ester at equilibrium. *(2 marks)*

**d.** Repeat with 1.00 mol of acid and 2.00 mol of ethanol. Explain the change with Le Chatelier's principle. *(3 marks)*

**e.** Calculate the atom economy of the esterification (M: ethanoic acid 60.0, ethanol 46.0, ethyl ethanoate 88.0). *(1 mark)*

**f.** Describe the ¹H NMR spectrum of ethyl ethanoate: the number of environments, their peak areas and their splitting. *(2 marks)*

**g.** How could IR spectroscopy show that the purified product contains no ethanoic acid? *(1 mark)*
